> AGENT

Yes. This looks like a real queue bug, not just operator confusion.

What happened is:

- The run is still mid-cycle. `plan_start_scores` is populated, so the tool considers the current cycle active.
- Mid-cycle scans intentionally skip cluster regeneration and triage injection to preserve queue state in [plan_reconcile.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/plan_reconcile.py#L494).
- Those same scans still update subjective deferral state in [plan_reconcile.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/plan_reconcile.py#L503) and [dimensions.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/sync/dimensions.py#L285).
- After 3 deferred scans, subjective items escalate by design: `DEFER_ESCALATE_AFTER_SCANS = 3` in [defer_policy.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/sync/defer_policy.py#L10), and escalated subjective IDs are force-promoted in [dimensions.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/sync/dimensions.py#L317).
- The execution queue then treats those `force_visible_ids` as tracked queue items in [core.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py#L255), and lifecycle filtering explicitly allows force-visible items through even during execute phase in [lifecycle.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/lifecycle.py#L184).

The inconsistent part is this:

- The execution queue currently has `0` objective actionable items.
- The broader backlog still has `55` objective items.
- So `next` is showing escalated subjective work while real objective work still exists off-queue.

That mismatch is the bug. The system is mixing two different notions of backlog:

- global objective backlog, which blocks triage and post-flight
- execution-queue visibility, which only shows tracked plan items

Because objective backlog stayed in backlog instead of the execution queue, the defer-escalation path made subjective items visible “too early”.

The live state confirms it:
- `scan_count = 57`
- `subjective_defer_meta.defer_count = 4`
- `first_deferred_scan = 54`
- `force_visible_ids = 20 subjective::* items`

So your intuition was right: subjective review work is appearing before objective work is actually drained. The root cause is not that review ran at the wrong time, but that the queue promotion logic can override the intended phase boundary when objective work exists only in backlog.

If you want, I can patch this next. The safest fix is probably: do not surface `force_visible` subjective items into the execution queue when execution has `0` tracked objective items but the broader backlog still has objective work.

```json
{
```
```json
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py\n@@\n def _planned_item_ids(plan: dict) -> set[str]:\n     \"\"\"Collect IDs explicitly tracked by the living plan.\"\"\"\n     tracked_ids: set[str] = set(plan.get(\"queue_order\", []))\n     tracked_ids.update(plan.get(\"skipped\", {}).keys())\n@@\n         tracked_ids.update(subjective_defer_meta.get(\"force_visible_ids\", []))\n     return tracked_ids\n \n \n+def _is_objective_queue_item(item: WorkQueueItem) -> bool:\n+    \"\"\"Return True when an item is actionable objective work.\"\"\"\n+    return (\n+        item.get(\"kind\") in (\"issue\", \"cluster\")\n+        and item.get(\"detector\", \"\") not in {\"subjective_assessment\", \"subjective_review\", \"review\", \"concerns\"}\n+    )\n+\n+\n def _filter_plan_visibility(\n     items: list[WorkQueueItem],\n     plan: dict | None,\n     *,\n     visibility: str,\n@@\n     if not tracked_ids:\n         if visibility == QueueVisibility.EXECUTION:\n             return [item for item in items if _is_implicit_execution_workflow(item)]\n         return items\n     if visibility == QueueVisibility.EXECUTION:\n-        return [\n+        visible = [\n             item for item in items\n             if item[\"id\"] in tracked_ids or _is_implicit_execution_workflow(item)\n         ]\n+        skipped_ids = set(plan.get(\"skipped\", {}).keys())\n+        has_tracked_objective = any(\n+            _is_objective_queue_item(item) and item[\"id\"] not in skipped_ids\n+            for item in visible\n+        )\n+        if has_tracked_objective:\n+            return visible\n+\n+        # When the tracked slice has drained objective work but the broader\n+        # backlog still has unplanned objective issues, execution must stay in\n+        # execute mode rather than surfacing post-flight subjective/workflow\n+        # items. This prevents mid-cycle scans plus force-visible reruns from\n+        # creating a false post-flight queue while mechanical work still exists.\n+        hidden_objective = [\n+            item for item in items\n+            if (\n+                _is_objective_queue_item(item)\n+                and item[\"id\"] not in tracked_ids\n+                and not _is_implicit_execution_workflow(item)\n+            )\n+        ]\n+        if hidden_objective:\n+            return hidden_objective\n+        return visible\n     if visibility == QueueVisibility.BACKLOG:\n         return [\n             item for item in items\n             if item[\"id\"] not in tracked_ids and not _is_implicit_execution_workflow(item)\n         ]\n*** End Patch\n"
}
```

> AGENT

The root cause was a policy mismatch, not a random leak.

- The execution queue only showed plan-tracked items.
- Mid-cycle scans intentionally do not regenerate the plan, so newly found mechanical issues stayed in backlog instead of entering execution.
- Separately, subjective reruns had a starvation escape hatch: after 3 deferred scans they became `force_visible`.
- That meant `next` could show force-visible subjective items from the tracked plan while real objective issues still existed off to the side in backlog.

So the invariant was broken by the interaction of two deliberate features:
- “execution queue = tracked plan slice”
- “force-visible subjective reruns can bypass normal gating”

I fixed the execution visibility logic in [core.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py#L271). Now, if the tracked execution slice has no actionable objective items left but unplanned objective backlog still exists, execution stays in `execute` and surfaces that objective backlog instead of post-flight subjective/workflow items. I added a regression test for exactly this state in [test_work_queue_plan_order_and_triage.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/review/test_work_queue_plan_order_and_triage.py#L314).

Verification:
- `pytest -q desloppify/tests/review/test_work_queue_plan_order_and_triage.py`
- `pytest -q desloppify/tests/commands/test_queue_count_consistency.py`
- `pytest -q desloppify/tests/commands/scan/test_plan_reconcile_postflight_and_reconcile.py`

Live check against your current `.desloppify` state:
- before: execution queue was `20 subjective + 1 workflow`, `0` objective actionable
- now: execution queue is `55` objective items, `0` subjective, phase `execute`
- `desloppify next --count 10` now starts with mechanical findings again, not subjective review items

The underlying issue was real: execution and lifecycle were using different definitions of “backlog”. This patch makes `next` obey the phase boundary you described.

> DEVELOPER

Why was this possible in the first place? Is there confusing engineering?

> AGENT

Yes. The bug was possible because the queue model had two conflicting truths.

- “Objective backlog exists” was computed globally from state.
- “What `next` should show” was computed from the execution queue, which only included plan-tracked items.

That split is the core engineering problem.

Concretely:

- The execution queue intentionally hid unplanned issues in [core.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py#L279).
- Mid-cycle scans intentionally avoided regenerating clusters/plan structure in [plan_reconcile.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/plan_reconcile.py#L483), so new mechanical issues could remain unplanned.
- Subjective reruns had an escalation mechanism after repeated defers in [defer_policy.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/sync/defer_policy.py#L10) and [dimensions.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/sync/dimensions.py#L285).
- `force_visible` then bypassed lifecycle gating in [lifecycle.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/lifecycle.py#L165).

So the system could simultaneously believe:

- “objective backlog is still open”
- “execution queue has no objective work”
- “subjective reruns are now force-visible”

That should have been treated as impossible, but it wasn’t, because those decisions lived in different modules with different scopes.

So yes, there is confusing engineering here. The main confusion points are:

- `backlog` means different things in different places: global state backlog, execution queue backlog, and plan-tracked backlog.
- lifecycle is resolved from visible queue items, not from the full state.
- mid-cycle scan preservation is sensible by itself, but it interacts badly with deferred-subjective escalation.
- `force_visible` is too blunt: it bypassed the phase boundary instead of just preventing starvation inside the correct phase.

A strong design rule would be:

- post-flight items must never become execution-visible while any objective issue exists anywhere in active scope.

Before this fix, that rule was not encoded centrally. That was the real design flaw.

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

I have enough repo truth to frame the redesign. One decision changes the size of the plan a lot: whether to keep the living-plan/backlog split and harden it, or simplify around a single canonical queue model.

> DEVELOPER

Missing from implementation details                                                                                                  
                                                                                                                                       
  1. Two resolve_lifecycle_phase functions exist today — one in lifecycle.py (takes items) and one in refresh_lifecycle.py (takes      
  explicit facts). The plan says "centralize phase resolution" but doesn't specify which becomes canonical or how they merge. Since    
  QueueSnapshot is the new source of truth, the items-based one in lifecycle.py should probably go away entirely, with the snapshot    
  computing the facts that refresh_lifecycle.py's version needs.  
                                                                                                                                       
  2. Relationship to existing QueueContext — context.py already has a QueueContext dataclass that commands consume. The plan doesn't   
  mention it. Is QueueSnapshot a replacement, an extension, or a separate thing that QueueContext wraps?                               
                                                                                                                                       
  3. SubjectiveVisibility dataclass — currently computes has_objective_backlog, objective_count, and various ID sets. This overlaps    
  heavily with QueueSnapshot fields. Need to decide: does QueueSnapshot subsume it, or does SubjectiveVisibility feed into
  QueueSnapshot construction?                                                                                                          
                                                                  
  4. Synthetic item generation — synthetic.py and synthetic_workflow.py generate the subjective, triage, and workflow queue items.     
  QueueSnapshot needs to be built after these are generated. The plan doesn't mention this dependency or whether synthetic generation
  itself needs changes.                                                                                                                
                                                                  
  5. Plan.json migration — the plan says "preserve plan.json compatibility" but removing force_visible_ids from subjective_defer_meta  
  and triage_force_visible from epic_triage_meta is a schema change. Per the backward compat policy, you need data migration code to
  strip/ignore these fields gracefully on load.                                                                                        
                                                                  
  Ambiguities to resolve                                                                                                               
  
  6. Initial review vs execute priority — Phase priority #1 says "initial subjective review only if zero in-scope objective issues."   
  But today, lifecycle.py resolves review before execute (unassessed subjective dimensions take priority). Your plan reverses this. Is
  that intentional? If a first-time scan has both unscored subjective dimensions and objective issues, should the user go straight to  
  execute and defer initial review? That changes UX.              

  7. Snapshot lifecycle — when exactly is the snapshot built? Once per CLI command invocation? Some commands (like review) modify state
   mid-execution — do they need to rebuild the snapshot? If it's built once and cached, stale snapshot bugs become possible.
                                                                                                                                       
  8. Scope filtering order — cluster focus and scan_path scoping currently filter items within the queue pipeline. The plan says these 
  "must not change the global phase decision" (in test plan) but doesn't say where scoping sits relative to QueueSnapshot construction.
   If the snapshot is built from unscoped items but rendering applies scope, phase could disagree with what's shown. Need to be        
  explicit: snapshot is built from scoped items.                  

  9. Workflow items during execute — the plan excludes "workflow::run-scan" during execute but doesn't address other workflow items    
  (create-plan, etc.). Are all workflow items gated, or only post-flight ones?
                                                                                                                                       
  Things the test plan should also cover                                                                                               
  
  10. First scan / empty state — no plan exists yet, no issues scanned. What does QueueSnapshot look like? Phase should be scan,       
  execution empty.                                                
                                                                                                                                       
  11. Target-strict filtering — if target_strict filters out all objective issues from scope, does phase flip to review even though    
  unscoped objective work exists? This is an existing ambiguity the redesign should nail down.
                                                                                                                                       
  12. Concurrent plan mutations — if plan queue is being read while triage writes to plan.json, does the snapshot guarantee            
  consistency? Probably fine since it's single-process, but worth a note.
                                                                                                                                       
  ---                                                             
  The core invariant and the force_visible removal are the high-value parts. The main risk is #6 (initial review priority change) since
   it's a silent UX change, and #5 (migration) since old plan files will have the removed fields.

I’m going to lock that one down explicitly before I replace the plan.

> DEVELOPER

This is solid. Every concern from round one is addressed explicitly. A few remaining things:

  §4 has an internal contradiction. Step 5 says "synthesize only the item families legal for that phase," but the snapshot contains    
  fields for all partitions (execution, backlog, review, workflow, triage). Backlog is defined as "open work not currently admitted by
  the active phase" — so during execute, backlog should contain subjective/triage/workflow items. If you only synthesize items legal   
  for the active phase, backlog will be empty of those. You probably mean: synthesize all item families, but admit only phase-legal
  ones to execution. Clarify whether synthetic generation is phase-gated or just partitioning is.

  Phase granularity change needs a persistence note. You're introducing review_initial, review_postflight, workflow_postflight,        
  triage_postflight as distinct phases. Currently plan.json persists a single lifecycle_phase string from a 5-value enum. Are these all
   new persisted values, or do some collapse for storage (e.g., review_initial and review_postflight both persist as "review")? This   
  affects plan.json compatibility.                                

  "Fresh boundary" needs a definition. §6 says initial review triggers "at a fresh boundary" but doesn't define what that means. First 
  scan ever? First scan of a new plan cycle? After a plan reset? This matters because it determines whether initial review re-triggers
  after, say, a plan reset mid-execution.                                                                                              
                                                                  
  Test plan gap: empty/first-run state. No plan, no state, no scan yet. QueueSnapshot should produce phase scan with everything empty. 
  Worth an explicit test case.
                                                                                                                                       
  Otherwise, this is ready to implement. The pipeline ordering in §4 (steps 1-7), the scope/focus distinction in §8, and the migration 
  strategy are all clean.

> AGENT

Implemented the queue redesign around a canonical snapshot path.

Core changes:
- Added [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py) with `QueueSnapshot`, internal phase resolution, execution/backlog partitioning, and coarse persisted phase mapping.
- Wired [context.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/context.py), [core.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py), [queue_policy.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/planning/queue_policy.py), [queue_progress.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/queue_progress.py), and [plan_reconcile.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/plan_reconcile.py) to use snapshot-derived phase/visibility instead of the old mixed lifecycle path.
- Removed behavioral use of legacy `force_visible` data. Load/write compatibility is kept only in [__init__.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema/__init__.py), which strips `subjective_defer_meta.force_visible_ids` and `epic_triage_meta.triage_force_visible`.
- Tightened review rerun gating in [preflight.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/preflight.py) to use snapshot facts while not letting a targeted subjective queue item block its own rerun.
- Removed the new import cycle by simplifying [triage_start_policy.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/sync/triage_start_policy.py) and cutting `plan_queue` coupling in [synthetic.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/synthetic.py).

Behaviorally, this now enforces the invariant you wanted:
- execution phase is driven by any in-scope objective backlog
- unplanned objective blockers surface in execution instead of being hidden in backlog
- stale subjective, triage, and post-flight workflow items no longer leak into `next` during execute
- scan phase shows `workflow::deferred-disposition` before `workflow::run-scan`, never both in execution

Verified with:
```bash
pytest -q desloppify/tests/engine/test_queue_context.py desloppify/tests/engine/test_sync_split_modules_direct.py desloppify/tests/review/test_work_queue_plan_order_and_triage.py desloppify/tests/commands/test_queue_count_consistency.py desloppify/tests/commands/review/test_review_preflight.py desloppify/tests/commands/test_queue_progress.py desloppify/tests/commands/scan/test_plan_reconcile_postflight_and_reconcile.py desloppify/tests/commands/test_next_helpers_direct.py desloppify/tests/commands/test_next_queue_flow_direct.py desloppify/tests/commands/plan/test_saved_plan_recovery.py
pytest -q desloppify/tests/commands/test_lifecycle_transitions.py desloppify/tests/review/work_queue_cases.py desloppify/tests/plan/test_plan_modules_direct.py
```
`270` tests passed.

There were unrelated pre-existing worktree changes in files like [cli.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli.py), [test_cli.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/test_cli.py), and [website/main.js](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/website/main.js). I left those alone.

> DEVELOPER

Is this whole area beautifula nd well-engineered now?

> AGENT

No.

It is materially better and the core invariant is now encoded, but I would not call this area beautiful yet.

What is better:
- Phase and visibility decisions now come from one snapshot path in [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py), which is the right architectural center.
- The main bug class is gone: post-flight items no longer leak into execute because of plan metadata.
- The queue/preflight/reconcile paths now agree much more consistently.

What is still not clean:
- There is still dead or semi-dead lifecycle logic in [lifecycle.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/lifecycle.py). Production no longer depends on it, but it still exists and still has tests around it. That is confusing engineering.
- [plan_queue.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/plan_queue.py) is still a broad facade re-exporting old concepts. It is less dangerous now, but it still makes dependency boundaries harder to read.
- `QueueContext` still carries both `policy` and `snapshot` in [context.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/context.py). That is acceptable for transition, but it means there are still two overlapping summary objects in memory.
- [core.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py) is better than before, but it still does too much: input resolution, canonical queue selection, legacy issue-list behavior, plan ordering, and final rendering prep.

My assessment:
- Correctness: much better
- Invariant clarity: good
- Boundary hygiene: improved, not finished
- Beauty: not yet
- Maintenance risk: reduced, but still moderate until the old lifecycle path and broad facades are deleted

If you want the next step, I’d do a cleanup pass with two explicit goals:
- delete [lifecycle.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/lifecycle.py) and migrate its remaining tests to snapshot behavior
- narrow [plan_queue.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/plan_queue.py) so it stops being a grab-bag facade

That would move it from “fixed and defensible” to “well-engineered.”

> AGENT

It is cleaner now, and the confusing compatibility layers are gone.

What changed:
- Deleted the dead items-based lifecycle module: [lifecycle.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/lifecycle.py)
- Deleted the broad queue facade: [plan_queue.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/plan_queue.py)
- Switched remaining production imports to primary modules under `engine._plan`, `engine._work_queue`, `engine.planning`, and `engine.plan_state` / `engine.plan_ops` / `engine.plan_triage`
- Replaced the old lifecycle tests with snapshot-behavior tests in [test_sync_split_modules_direct.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/engine/test_sync_split_modules_direct.py)
- Updated the plan-surface contract test to forbid the old `engine.plan_queue` hub rather than preserving it as an allowed surface

So the area is now closer to “well-engineered”:
- one canonical queue model in [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py)
- no dead lifecycle API pretending to matter
- no broad queue barrel masking boundaries
- queue, preflight, reconcile, and import paths use the same underlying facts

I still wouldn’t call it perfect. [core.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py) is still a bit dense, and `QueueContext` still carries both `policy` and `snapshot`, which is transitional rather than ideal. But the architecture is now coherent instead of contradictory.

Verification:
- `336` tests passed across queue, review, import, reconcile, lifecycle-transition, and plan-surface suites.

Unrelated pre-existing worktree changes are still present in files like [cli.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli.py), [test_cli.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/test_cli.py), and [website/main.js](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/website/main.js). I left those alone.

is this now beautiful?

> AGENT

Closer, but not fully.

I’d call it:
- architecturally coherent
- defensible
- much less confusing
- not yet elegant

Why it’s better now:
- there is one real queue authority in [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py)
- the fake compatibility surfaces are gone
- phase/visibility behavior is encoded directly instead of emerging from conflicting modules

Why it’s still not beautiful:
- [core.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py) still does too much
- [context.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/context.py) still carries both `policy` and `snapshot`
- there are still a lot of direct imports from `engine._plan`, which is fine technically but not especially clean as a public architecture story

So: it is now good engineering. It is not yet beautiful engineering.

If you want the next step toward “beautiful,” I’d do one focused cleanup:
- split [core.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py) into `inputs`, `selection`, and `finalize`
- then either fold `SubjectiveVisibility` into the snapshot builder or make it clearly subordinate to snapshot instead of co-equal

That would be the pass where it starts to feel elegant rather than merely correct.

plan, opts)\n+\n+    total = len(items)\n+    if opts.count is not None and opts.count > 0:\n+        items = items[:opts.count]\n+    if opts.explain:\n+        for item in items:\n+            item[\"explain\"] = item_explain(item)\n+\n+    return {\n+        \"items\": items,\n+        \"total\": total,\n+        \"grouped\": group_queue_items(items, \"item\"),\n+        \"new_ids\": new_ids,\n+    }\n+\n+\n+def _plan_presort(\n+    items: list[WorkQueueItem],\n+    state: StateModel,\n+    plan: dict | None,\n+) -> tuple[set[str], list[WorkQueueItem]]:\n+    \"\"\"Enrich plan metadata and stamp sort keys before sorting.\"\"\"\n+    if not plan:\n+        return set(), []\n+\n+    new_ids = _new_item_ids(state)\n+    enrich_plan_metadata(items, plan)\n+    stamp_plan_sort_keys(items, plan, new_ids)\n+    remaining, skipped = separate_skipped(items, plan)\n+    items[:] = remaining\n+    return new_ids, skipped\n+\n+\n+def _plan_postsort(\n+    items: list[WorkQueueItem],\n+    skipped: list[WorkQueueItem],\n+    plan: dict | None,\n+    opts: QueueBuildOptions,\n+) -> None:\n+    \"\"\"Re-append skipped items and stamp positions.\"\"\"\n+    if not plan:\n+        return\n+    if opts.include_skipped:\n+        items.extend(skipped)\n+    stamp_positions(items, plan)\n+\n+\n+__all__ = [\"finalize_queue\"]\n*** End Patch\n"
```json
}
```

The targeted failure exposed a real semantic leak: `objective_execution_count` was counting `workflow::run-scan` as if it were objective work. I’m fixing that field now rather than weakening the test.

> TOOL

tool_use apply_patch
```json
{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/engine/test_queue_context.py\n@@\n class TestQueueContextPolicy:\n-    def test_policy_computed_with_resolved_params(self):\n-        \"\"\"Policy uses the resolved plan and target_strict.\"\"\"\n+    def test_snapshot_respects_resolved_plan(self):\n+        \"\"\"Snapshot uses the resolved plan and target_strict.\"\"\"\n         state = _state_with_issues(_issue(\"f1\"))\n         plan = {\"skipped\": {\"f1\": {\"kind\": \"temporary\"}}}\n         ctx = queue_context(state, plan=plan, target_strict=80.0)\n-        # f1 is skipped by plan, so objective_count should be 0\n-        assert ctx.policy.objective_count == 0\n-        assert ctx.policy.has_objective_backlog is False\n+        assert ctx.snapshot.objective_in_scope_count == 0\n+        assert ctx.snapshot.objective_execution_count == 0\n \n-    def test_policy_counts_objective_issues(self):\n-        \"\"\"Policy correctly counts open objective issues.\"\"\"\n+    def test_snapshot_counts_objective_issues(self):\n+        \"\"\"Snapshot correctly counts open objective issues.\"\"\"\n         state = _state_with_issues(\n             _issue(\"f1\"),\n             _issue(\"f2\"),\n         )\n         ctx = queue_context(state, plan=None)\n-        assert ctx.policy.objective_count == 2\n-        assert ctx.policy.has_objective_backlog is True\n+        assert ctx.snapshot.objective_in_scope_count == 2\n+        assert ctx.snapshot.objective_execution_count == 2\n \n-    def test_policy_excludes_subjective_detectors(self):\n-        \"\"\"Issues from subjective detectors don't count as objective.\"\"\"\n+    def test_snapshot_excludes_subjective_detectors(self):\n+        \"\"\"Issues from subjective detectors don't count as objective.\"\"\"\n         state = _state_with_issues(\n             _issue(\"f1\", detector=\"unused\"),\n             _issue(\"f2\", detector=\"review\"),\n         )\n         ctx = queue_context(state, plan=None)\n-        assert ctx.policy.objective_count == 1\n+        assert ctx.snapshot.objective_in_scope_count == 1\n*** End Patch\n"
```

- Queue assembly is split across [inputs.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/inputs.py), [selection.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/selection.py), [finalize.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/finalize.py), and [models.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/models.py).
- [context.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/context.py) now carries the canonical [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py) instead of a co-equal queue policy object.

> DEVELOPER

Is this a bug to fix? Or something we should imrpove?

> AGENT

Yes. This is primarily a bug, with one secondary product improvement.

The bug is a source-of-truth mismatch in triage completion:
- Completion validates against frozen triage IDs from [`helpers.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py) via `active_triage_issue_ids()` and `undispositioned_triage_issue_ids()`.
- `unclustered_review_issues()` in [`stages/helpers.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stages/helpers.py) explicitly re-injects frozen IDs that are no longer open in state.
- But normal disposition commands like `plan skip` target live matching issues, so once those review findings are no longer open, the CLI can stop matching them.
- Result: triage can get stuck requiring dispositions for IDs that no longer exist as actionable issues.

That is not just “could be nicer.” It is an invalid state machine. The user can be blocked from completing triage with no normal command path to satisfy the validator.

The improvement is the recovery UX:
- triage should either auto-prune frozen IDs that are no longer open, or
- `plan skip` / triage disposition commands should be able to target frozen `active_triage_issue_ids`, not only live open issues, or
- completion should offer a repair path like “drop stale frozen triage IDs”.

So my answer is:
- `bug`: yes, definitely
- `improvement`: also yes, but only after fixing the correctness bug

The plan-file patch I applied was a workaround, not the proper fix.

> DEVELOPER

is that a good fix? Will it help others avoid this? is it getting to the root of the problem?

> AGENT

No. The `plan.json` edit is a workaround, not a good fix.

It helps this repo instance get unstuck, but:
- it will not help other users
- it does not fix the code path that created the bad state
- it leaves the system able to produce the same blockage again

The root problem is deeper:
- triage freezes a review issue set in [`active_triage_issue_ids()`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py)
- completion validates against that frozen set
- but disposition commands operate on live/open issues
- and [`unclustered_review_issues()`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stages/helpers.py) can still treat no-longer-open frozen IDs as needing action

That means the model mixes:
- “issues this triage run started with”
- “issues currently open in state”
- “issues the CLI can still target”

Those should not be allowed to diverge without a repair rule.

A real fix should be in code, probably here:
- [`undispositioned_triage_issue_ids()`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py)
- [`unclustered_review_issues()`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stages/helpers.py)

What I’d change:
- only require dispositions for `active_triage_issue_ids ∩ currently_open_review_ids`
- treat frozen IDs that are no longer open as resolved/stale, not blocking
- regenerate `undispositioned_issue_ids` from live state instead of trusting saved stale metadata
- optionally print a note like “N frozen triage issues disappeared since triage started; they were dropped from completion validation”

That would get to the root and prevent this for others.

So:
- manual plan patch: useful emergency unblock
- real product fix: no, not yet
- root cause addressed: no, not by the patch

If you want, I can implement the actual code fix now.

> AGENT

I fixed the real bug.

The root fix is in [helpers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py) and [helpers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stages/helpers.py): triage completion now only blocks on `frozen triage IDs ∩ currently open review IDs`. Frozen IDs that disappeared from live state are no longer treated as undispositioned or unclustered action items. That gets to the actual mismatch between frozen triage state and live issue matching.

I added regressions in [test_triage_completion_cleanup.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/plan/test_triage_completion_cleanup.py) and [test_triage_stage_helpers_direct.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/plan/test_triage_stage_helpers_direct.py). `58` targeted tests passed, and the live command path now completes cleanly: `desloppify plan triage --complete --strategy "same"` succeeded.

> AGENT

Yes. I found `2` concrete adjacent issues, and `1` lower-risk cleanup smell.

- High: review-import pruning only cleans queue containers, not triage metadata. In [reconcile_review_import.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/reconcile_review_import.py#L58), `_prune_stale_review_ids_from_plan()` removes stale review IDs from `queue_order`, `deferred`, `skipped`, and cluster `issue_ids`, but it does not touch `epic_triage_meta.active_triage_issue_ids`, `triaged_ids`, `dismissed_ids`, or the persisted `undispositioned_*` fields. That is the same bug family: live state moves, persisted triage state does not. The completion bug we just fixed can reappear from this path unless metadata pruning is added here too.

- Medium: completion still persists the frozen triage set into `triaged_ids`, even if some of those IDs are no longer open. In [helpers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py#L417), `_coverage_open_ids()` prefers `active_triage_issue_ids`, and [helpers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py#L441) writes that set straight into `meta["triaged_ids"]`. Then [prompt.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/triage/prompt.py#L118) computes `new_since_last` / `resolved_since_last` against that stored set. That no longer blocks completion after my fix, but it can still skew triage history and “resolved since last triage” reporting.

- Low: `stage_refresh_required` / `stage_snapshot_hash` look like stale plumbing. They are displayed in [layout.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/display/layout.py#L78) and cleared in [helpers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py#L480), but I did not find a current writer setting them. That is not the same correctness bug, but it is confusing engineering in the same pipeline.

So the short answer is: yes, there is still one real root-cause bug left in the pipeline, and one follow-on baseline bug. The next fix I would do is the first one in [reconcile_review_import.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/reconcile_review_import.py#L58), because that is the place stale review IDs should be pruned from triage metadata as well as queue containers.

> DEVELOPER

Why did the subjecteive review ietms come to the front of the queue? My understand was that this should only happen when all the rest of the queue is cleared

> AGENT

Yes. Your understanding is the intended one.

Subjective review items should only surface:
- at the fresh-cycle initial review boundary, or
- after all in-scope objective work is drained

Why they came to the front anyway:
- The old queue logic treated “execution queue” and “objective backlog” as different things.
- Mid-cycle scans were preserving the current plan instead of regenerating it, so newly found objective work could stay in backlog without entering the visible execution queue.
- At the same time, stale subjective items had a defer/escalation path that could force them visible after repeated defers.
- So the system reached a contradictory state:
  - global objective work still existed
  - visible execution queue showed no objective work
  - subjective reruns were allowed to surface

That is why subjective items appeared before the queue was actually cleared. It was a real bug.

The core bug was in the old split between:
- plan-tracked visible queue items
- broader in-scope backlog

That is what I fixed with the canonical snapshot path in [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py) and the queue entrypoint split in [core.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py). Now phase/visibility is based on all in-scope objective work, not just whatever happened to be sitting in the tracked queue slice.

There was a second, separate bug in triage:
- frozen triage IDs could outlive live review issues and block completion
- that one was in the triage pipeline, not the queue-ordering logic
- I fixed that in [helpers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py), [helpers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stages/helpers.py), and [reconcile_review_import.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/reconcile_review_import.py)

So:
- subjective items reaching the front early: queue-phase bug
- triage later getting stuck on old review IDs: stale triage-metadata bug

Both were violations of the same general rule: persisted planning metadata was being trusted after live state had changed.

So the flow is:

2. Resolve it with `desloppify plan resolve ...`.
3. When the active queue is empty, run `desloppify scan`.
4. That post-flight scan should then surface the next review/triage/post-flight work properly.

So “what’s happening now” is: triage is complete, the queue bug is no longer front-loading subjective items, and you are down to one concrete objective blocker before the next scan boundary.

IS there anything in it that could make teh code worse?

> AGENT

I’m checking the concrete top item and the highest-risk review findings, because the danger here is not “bad queue order” so much as “good themes that could be implemented in a destructive way.”

> AGENT

Yes, a few items are risky in the sense that a naive “fix” could make the code worse.

The current active item is the clearest example:
- [unused.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/typescript/detectors/unused.py#L60) has a `B607` warning for `subprocess.run(["npx", "tsc", ...])`.
- That warning is real in the Bandit sense, but this is internal tooling code, not a hostile-input execution path.
- A bad fix here would be hardcoding an `npx` path or otherwise reducing portability just to silence the warning.

The review backlog also has several “good diagnosis, risky implementation” items:
- `queue_render_wrapper_shape_drift` in [queue_flow.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/next/queue_flow.py): collapsing wrappers too aggressively could make the command surface less readable, not more.
- `state_facade_still_codebase_hub` around [state.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/state.py): deleting facades too fast can spread imports everywhere and worsen boundaries before replacements exist.
- `plan_schema_migrations_blend_repair_and_version_steps` in [migrations.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema/migrations.py): splitting this carelessly could make recovery behavior less robust.
- `legacy_facades_still_primary_runtime_surface`: same story; migration work can easily increase churn and break callers if done as a purity exercise.

So my read is:
- The queue is not obviously poisoned.
- But some items are “improve architecture” tasks where a shallow cleanup would absolutely make the code worse.
- The right bar is: fix the behavior and narrow the boundary, not “remove wrappers/facades because wrappers/facades are ugly.”

If you want, I can go item by item and mark which ones look like:
- real bug fix
- worthwhile cleanup
- likely low-value/noisy
- risky unless tightly scoped

> AGENT

Yes. I cleaned the plan rather than just hand-waving about it.

What I changed:
- Tightened the `triage-runner-simplification` cluster description so it matches the actual work inside it.
- Set explicit cluster priorities so the post-scan plan favors correctness and contract work over broader wrapper/facade cleanup.

The improved intended order is:
1. `plan-sync-and-cluster-state`
2. `review-seed-and-import-contracts`
3. `review-batch-type-contracts`
4. `queue-surfaces-and-tests`
5. `state-and-facade-migration`
6. `triage-runner-simplification`

That is a better shape for “beautiful” because it puts semantics, lifecycle correctness, and truthful contracts ahead of aesthetic simplification.

One thing still looks off in the tool itself:
- `desloppify plan cluster list` reflects the new explicit priorities
- `desloppify plan queue --include-skipped` still renders the older banner order

So the plan data is cleaner now, but there is probably still a queue-render ordering inconsistency in the CLI surface. The immediate active item is still the same workflow boundary: run the post-flight scan. After that, the cleaned plan should be a safer guide than it was before.
